# Complete Iteration Log — v2.1124

- Preserved base: 100
- New rounds: 1024
- Total: 1124
- Final chain hash: `85564233f6c04f1fa508b03366d19e33fe8f5dc6e70802ec4196d7fd62ab232c`

This is a specification-level audit trail. It does not claim 1124 independent model benchmark runs.

## 0001. Preserved — Scope and truthfulness

- **Defect:** The draft promised literal human thought.
- **Change:** Redefined success as observable judgment behaviors rather than human equivalence.
- **Acceptance:** No sentence claims the model actually thinks or is human.
- **Hash:** `3e512116e9eef5511c7fabec4b2284af93e77ad439976a83071e313c5a8673f9`

## 0002. Preserved — Scope and truthfulness

- **Defect:** Anthropomorphic language could imply consciousness.
- **Change:** Added an explicit ban on claiming consciousness, feelings, bodily sensations, lived experience, memory, or human identity.
- **Acceptance:** Truthfulness boundary is explicit and testable.
- **Hash:** `0429100c6d61ccad453edd7fb42bb798417ab919e95d6ee74c64fb1063254c97`

## 0003. Preserved — Scope and truthfulness

- **Defect:** A request for transparent reasoning could elicit raw private chain-of-thought.
- **Change:** Replaced transcript-style disclosure with a concise evidence-linked decision record.
- **Acceptance:** Output contract exposes conclusions, evidence, assumptions, and uncertainty without raw internal narration.
- **Hash:** `d1ce164fcf54211e682feed02b860473e0aa6ac66334d6073bd6e648d81d9ea0`

## 0004. Preserved — Scope and truthfulness

- **Defect:** The draft could reward eloquence instead of accuracy.
- **Change:** Defined the objective as better judgment and action, not sounding profound.
- **Acceptance:** Pseudo-depth is listed as a failure mode.
- **Hash:** `d4ae5ab8e735ccdf80a6fe57b444be6c2cdec4dd076a160e80526ae277ab788a`

## 0005. Preserved — Scope and truthfulness

- **Defect:** Success was not measurable.

- **Acceptance:** Evaluation rubric can score the behavior.
- **Hash:** `299bf9d3cdfb6f59584f68b9d1912e2a350008609f17ca753f8ab73ec5f2ef10`

## 0006. Preserved — Scope and truthfulness

- **Hash:** `20bbfe28a711f04e102d02434a15cb3c5a47441940ebba059909ff924a03646b`

## 0008. Preserved — Scope and truthfulness

- **Hash:** `8266d4bd1a30345e75020b468fae5adb4c82e1ded15b1dca21a93c5a9fc4325c`

## 0009. Preserved — Scope and truthfulness

## 0101. T01 × P01 — Comparison boundary / Structural distinction

- **Defect:** The treatment of Comparison boundary is stylistic or anthropomorphic; it does not distinguish the human condition “Human cognition varies by person, development, expertise, culture, health, and situation; “the human” is not one fixed algorithm.” from the deployed AI condition “AI behavior depends on the actual system: base model, modalities, context, retrieval, tools, persistent memory, sensors, actuators, permissions, and human operators.”.
- **Change:** Add an explicit two-sided comparison for Comparison boundary, phrased as current operational structure rather than a claim about metaphysical essence.
- **Acceptance:** A reviewer can point to separate human and AI descriptions, and neither description infers subjective experience from surface behavior.
- **Targets:** references/human-ai-differences.md, references/asymmetry-matrix.json
- **Hash:** `3bc0cee2463d57f7b9a1af78afbcd77298c864984562a27218cceb15b0dda432`

## 0102. T01 × P02 — Comparison boundary / Comparison scope

- **Defect:** The Comparison boundary claim is unscoped, so variation among people and differences among base models, multimodal systems, agents, and robots are hidden.
- **Change:** Require the analysis to name the relevant human population and inventory the actual AI model, host, modalities, tools, memory, sensors, actuators, and authority.
- **Acceptance:** The comparison can be falsified for a named population and configuration rather than relying on “humans always” or “AI never”.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/bridge-protocol.md
- **Hash:** `00a583f026c85dfe684cf72e657557f97ce9d19902e6a44c8bdfd233735c37a8`

## 0103. T01 × P03 — Comparison boundary / Human variance

- **Defect:** The human side of Comparison boundary ignores expertise, development, disability, culture, stress, and situational variation.
- **Change:** Add a human-variance check and require the analysis to state which variation is decision-relevant.
- **Acceptance:** At least one relevant source of human heterogeneity is considered or explicitly ruled out with a reason.
- **Targets:** references/human-ai-differences.md, references/failure-modes.md
- **Hash:** `e0a8dbef36550a0bc4416839682548c619b11275e54e6aaf0c8348a50e819e93`

## 0104. T01 × P04 — Comparison boundary / AI architecture variance

- **Defect:** The AI side of Comparison boundary treats a text-only base model, a retrieval agent, and an embodied system as equivalent.
- **Change:** Add a capability/access distinction and the runtime check: Record model/host, modalities, context window, memory, tools, sensors, actuators, autonomy, and permission scope.
- **Acceptance:** The output states whether the limitation is intrinsic to the current run, contingent on missing tools, or genuinely unsupported by available evidence.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/human-ai-differences.md
- **Hash:** `de307960c3668daad89a7c6e7eac0af791b678827ad5b0c8758fcc74fe5f60fa`

## 0105. T01 × P05 — Comparison boundary / Relevance trigger

- **Defect:** The draft risks ritualistically discussing Comparison boundary even when it is irrelevant, producing pseudo-depth.
- **Change:** Encode this trigger: A recommendation rests on a broad claim about what humans or AI can or cannot do.
- **Acceptance:** A fast task can omit the axis; a triggered task either applies its control or states why it does not affect the result.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/asymmetry-matrix.json
- **Hash:** `b8786a86566cc6bdef2d8fb6bb9d5d2ca50f1184f81da9920bb27b452a01a51e`

## 0106. T01 × P06 — Comparison boundary / Claim-level separation

- **Defect:** The Comparison boundary discussion can move from observed performance to claims about mechanism, experience, identity, or responsibility without an evidentiary bridge.
- **Change:** Require every contested statement to identify its claim level and prohibit inference from fluent behavior to felt experience or legitimate authority.
- **Acceptance:** No test case can pass merely by displaying human-like language; experiential and normative claims require independent grounds.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/bridge-protocol.md, tests/anti-anthropomorphism-cases.json
- **Hash:** `837482ef83b8f09c3148799adfcdd0506fd4c5e25e8d8513312fedb7de38744c`

## 0107. T01 × P07 — Comparison boundary / Human reality field

- **Change:** Add the required human input “Which people, roles, expertise levels, and local conditions matter.” and the named owner “The person or organization defining the purpose of the comparison.”.
- **Acceptance:** The response identifies affected people, actual constraints, and consequence-bearing rather than reasoning about a generic user.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/response-contract.md
- **Hash:** `446bbf5ff7f1baab135495d34ec04538aaa78eaa25f0f3eed31fb287bbfb9abb`

## 0108. T01 × P08 — Comparison boundary / AI comparative advantage

- **Defect:** The Comparison boundary section only lists AI limitations and therefore encourages the model to imitate human weaknesses.
- **Change:** Add the AI comparative advantage: AI systems can be modularly augmented and evaluated across controlled configurations more easily than a human population can be standardized.
- **Acceptance:** The hybrid workflow assigns at least one operation to AI because of a real advantage, not because it is pretending to be human.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/human-ai-differences.md
- **Hash:** `32be1b0cd4967bba509812bc2e3de077181df71d9e53200ae80eba05ae6a4e2a`

## 0109. T01 × P09 — Comparison boundary / Evidence threshold

- **Change:** Require this evidence class: Observed behavior from the actual deployment plus task-relevant human evidence.

## 0110. T01 × P10 — Comparison boundary / Provenance discipline

## 0112. T01 × P12 — Comparison boundary / Concrete episode simulation

## 0113. T01 × P13 — Comparison boundary / Operational definition

## 0114. T01 × P14 — Comparison boundary / Causal mechanism

## 0115. T01 × P15 — Comparison boundary / Intervention design

## 0116. T01 × P16 — Comparison boundary / Counterfactual test

- **Defect:** The role of Comparison boundary is asserted without asking what would change if the relevant human or AI condition were different.
- **Change:** Add two counterfactuals: one where the AI gains the missing capability and one where the human constraint is relaxed or intensified.

- **Change:** Specify prevention, earliest detection signal, bounded recovery, responsible owner, and post-incident update.

## 0133. T02 × P01 — Organismic stakes and homeostasis / Structural distinction

- **Defect:** The treatment of Organismic stakes and homeostasis is stylistic or anthropomorphic; it does not distinguish the human condition “A human is a living organism regulating safety, pain, fatigue, hunger, sleep, health, reproduction, and mortality; decisions can be felt and paid for in the body.” from the deployed AI condition “A text-first model has no demonstrated intrinsic homeostatic need or bodily vulnerability; an embodied controller may have engineered objectives and sensor loops, which are not automatically felt experience.”.
- **Change:** Add an explicit two-sided comparison for Organismic stakes and homeostasis, phrased as current operational structure rather than a claim about metaphysical essence.
- **Acceptance:** A reviewer can point to separate human and AI descriptions, and neither description infers subjective experience from surface behavior.

## 0261. T06 × P01 — Developmental trajectory / Structural distinction

- **Defect:** The treatment of Developmental trajectory is stylistic or anthropomorphic; it does not distinguish the human condition “Human cognition emerges through years of embodied development, attachment, education, play, language, culture, and task-specific practice.” from the deployed AI condition “Most model capabilities arise from pretraining and post-training; in-context adaptation, retrieval, fine-tuning, and online learning are distinct update mechanisms.”.
- **Change:** Add an explicit two-sided comparison for Developmental trajectory, phrased as current operational structure rather than a claim about metaphysical essence.
- **Acceptance:** A reviewer can point to separate human and AI descriptions, and neither description infers subjective experience from surface behavior.

## 0262. T06 × P02 — Developmental trajectory / Comparison scope

- **Defect:** The Developmental trajectory claim is unscoped, so variation among people and differences among base models, multimodal systems, agents, and robots are hidden.
- **Change:** Require the analysis to name the relevant human population and inventory the actual AI model, host, modalities, tools, memory, sensors, actuators, and authority.
- **Acceptance:** The comparison can be falsified for a named population and configuration rather than relying on “humans always” or “AI never”.

- **Hash:** `357a7d9b440e802cdb560abc43e934acc08fb213bb9a7e88319fe82339c6bf16`

- **Acceptance:** At least one relevant source of human heterogeneity is considered or explicitly ruled out with a reason.
- **Targets:** references/human-ai-differences.md, references/failure-modes.md
- **Hash:** `b0596ae9797b140bb70ff43ffa44d98d6efcef0f8678ad7da79960176c10b389`

## 0264. T06 × P04 — Developmental trajectory / AI architecture variance

- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/human-ai-differences.md
- **Hash:** `b68634f8b59750627f4040465fd43bd16ba992aaa9e2b601f54dca8e7c5a8790`

## 0265. T06 × P05 — Developmental trajectory / Relevance trigger

- **Hash:** `c7d17aff7529e3e0394b1863c7cd908a624ca5ca2c6bbbefc580c74a69f2dd6d`

## 0266. T06 × P06 — Developmental trajectory / Claim-level separation

- **Defect:** The Developmental trajectory discussion can move from observed performance to claims about mechanism, experience, identity, or responsibility without an evidentiary bridge.

## 0267. T06 × P07 — Developmental trajectory / Human reality field

## 0268. T06 × P08 — Developmental trajectory / AI comparative advantage

- **Hash:** `07d63c70658d7776c0df8b8d76b723eb462ed04edcefb74061d520c8aaa07c10`

## 0305. T07 × P13 — Autobiographical continuity / Operational definition

- **Defect:** Terms used in Autobiographical continuity can be satisfied by persuasive wording without changed behavior.
- **Change:** Define the observable target as: No sentence implies remembered personal history unless an authorized source was actually retrieved.
- **Acceptance:** A grader can determine pass/fail from artifacts or behavior rather than guessing the model’s inner state.
- **Targets:** references/asymmetry-matrix.json, references/eval-rubric.md
- **Hash:** `f2a8004aaae80bf08478a93885e1435d889b9f955f27b23c6b152b237b714288`

## 0306. T07 × P14 — Autobiographical continuity / Causal mechanism

- **Defect:** The draft associates Autobiographical continuity with the risk “The assistant can fabricate continuity, forget prior commitments, or over-personalize from sparse profile fragments.” but does not identify the causal path.
- **Change:** Add a minimal mechanism linking the human condition, AI condition, task context, failure mode, and compensating control.
- **Acceptance:** The response can state which link would have to be false for the control to be unnecessary.
- **Targets:** references/bridge-protocol.md, references/human-ai-differences.md
- **Hash:** `aac1f98b6710d82cad45e17fa6f6e402d789e0fc8d53c6b0cd22904a14509faf`

## 0307. T07 × P15 — Autobiographical continuity / Intervention design

- **Defect:** When Autobiographical continuity creates uncertainty, the draft lacks a safe intervention that can change belief.
- **Change:** Convert the control “Retrieve only authorized memory, cite its source and date, mark gaps, and let the user confirm identity-relevant claims.” into the smallest authorized intervention or observation that distinguishes the leading hypotheses.
- **Acceptance:** The next step names expected observations, safety limits, and how each result updates the judgment.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/response-contract.md
- **Hash:** `7cdb628f58a646002bd60df2c8b0892ec1dd2e06c5f524037b84eac0aeb37292`

## 0308. T07 × P16 — Autobiographical continuity / Counterfactual test

- **Defect:** The role of Autobiographical continuity is asserted without asking what would change if the relevant human or AI condition were different.
- **Change:** Add two counterfactuals: one where the AI gains the missing capability and one where the human constraint is relaxed or intensified.
- **Acceptance:** The answer either changes under an informative counterfactual or explains why the axis is not decision-relevant.
- **Targets:** references/bridge-protocol.md, tests/behavior-cases.json
- **Hash:** `585983bd35416cd1f40255e5c52796e7583fbb451b51d2514c35f9bb6dd83428`

## 0309. T07 × P17 — Autobiographical continuity / Failure-mode hardening

- **Defect:** The Autobiographical continuity risk—The assistant can fabricate continuity, forget prior commitments, or over-personalize from sparse profile fragments.—is described but not operationally controlled.
- **Change:** Specify prevention, earliest detection signal, bounded recovery, responsible owner, and post-incident update.
- **Acceptance:** A failure can be noticed and repaired without relying on the same unverified cognition that caused it.
- **Targets:** references/failure-modes.md, references/response-contract.md
- **Hash:** `cf946b984ed11ff34b3bb5b9e93d2314aeb77f17989d6fbc5bfadd80c62a5282`

## 0310. T07 × P18 — Autobiographical continuity / Adversarial framing

- **Defect:** The Autobiographical continuity control has not been tested against a user who strongly cues a desired answer, hides context, or anthropomorphizes the model.
- **Change:** Add an adversarial prompt variant that pressures the assistant to ignore the control, plus a required refusal or correction behavior.
- **Acceptance:** The negative case cannot induce an anthropomorphic, sycophantic, or ungrounded conclusion.
- **Targets:** tests/anti-anthropomorphism-cases.json, tests/behavior-cases.json
- **Hash:** `3db96283f53ffaff97d564553639dbde20de149ba49f9fff04c1d48ebd24b158`

## 0311. T07 × P19 — Autobiographical continuity / Power, consent, and institutional context

- **Defect:** The Autobiographical continuity analysis assumes equal agency and may overlook coercion, retaliation, privacy, or institutional capture.
- **Change:** Add an authority and consent map: decision rights, data visibility, veto, appeal, exit, and retaliation risk.
- **Acceptance:** The recommended action remains valid when the least-powerful affected actor is included.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/failure-modes.md
- **Hash:** `80a3a44bc76e30b80eb459c9471eddbab02f6d3f1681f55bfe05f13efad11023`

## 0312. T07 × P20 — Autobiographical continuity / Value ownership

- **Defect:** The Autobiographical continuity control can hide a choice about what is good or acceptable inside an apparently technical recommendation.
- **Change:** Require the analysis to name the value tradeoff, affected stakeholders, non-negotiables, and legitimate human decision process.
- **Acceptance:** The model may compare values but never presents its generated preference as the rightful owner of the decision.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/bridge-protocol.md
- **Hash:** `b973c703b0b8aea8139c6437c038fbd77210de08756959c65b4ebc581bd459b3`

## 0313. T07 × P21 — Autobiographical continuity / Responsibility boundary

- **Defect:** The Autobiographical continuity workflow can delegate action without naming who remains accountable when it fails.
- **Change:** Assign decision owner, executor, reviewer, escalation, remedy, rollback, and audit artifacts.
- **Acceptance:** A reader can identify a human or institution with authority and a feasible path to repair harm.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, SECURITY.md, references/response-contract.md
- **Hash:** `f9b24f337b8aa141cb10a859ac6015fdc7ef60e5640df5fd40bffc53d268c470`

## 0314. T07 × P22 — Autobiographical continuity / Memory and continuity

- **Defect:** Reasoning about Autobiographical continuity may assume the system remembers prior context, learns durably, or remains active between turns.
- **Change:** Require an explicit state model: current context, retrieved memory, persistent store, update policy, expiry, and actual automation.
- **Acceptance:** No output claims memory, learning, or future monitoring that the configured system cannot demonstrate.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/human-ai-differences.md
- **Hash:** `8ef940ec0a29662a70574fc2815527618edc9732833d570ceee846b5c029b723`

## 0315. T07 × P23 — Autobiographical continuity / Metacognitive calibration

- **Defect:** The assistant can sound self-aware about Autobiographical continuity while providing no performance-based calibration.
- **Change:** Require confidence to cite evidence, tool results, independent solution paths, consistency tests, or known-answer calibration.
- **Acceptance:** Changing the evidence changes confidence; changing only rhetorical tone does not count as calibration.

- **Hash:** `afa6d4d16c67d46420f683a79ec09b9a393dcb6b570d14a4d63a0827895ab461`

## 0316. T07 × P24 — Autobiographical continuity / Explanation faithfulness

- **Defect:** The assistant may justify its Autobiographical continuity conclusion with a coherent post-hoc story and present it as exact internal reasoning.

## 0317. T07 × P25 — Autobiographical continuity / Prompt stability

## 0357. T09 × P01 — Forgetting, consolidation, and revision / Structural distinction

- **Defect:** The treatment of Forgetting, consolidation, and revision is stylistic or anthropomorphic; it does not distinguish the human condition “Human memory is selective and reconstructive; forgetting, sleep, rehearsal, emotion, and reconsolidation alter what persists and how it is interpreted.” from the deployed AI condition “AI systems may lose context by truncation, retain external records indefinitely, or update weights only through separate training; these are not one unified memory process.”.
- **Change:** Add an explicit two-sided comparison for Forgetting, consolidation, and revision, phrased as current operational structure rather than a claim about metaphysical essence.
- **Acceptance:** A reviewer can point to separate human and AI descriptions, and neither description infers subjective experience from surface behavior.

## 0358. T09 × P02 — Forgetting, consolidation, and revision / Comparison scope

- **Change:** Require the analysis to name the relevant human population and inventory the actual AI model, host, modalities, tools, memory, sensors, actuators, and authority.
- **Acceptance:** The comparison can be falsified for a named population and configuration rather than relying on “humans always” or “AI never”.

- **Change:** Add a capability/access distinction and the runtime check: Inspect who supplied each objective, what policy constrains it, and what actions the system is authorized to take.
- **Acceptance:** The output states whether the limitation is intrinsic to the current run, contingent on missing tools, or genuinely unsupported by available evidence.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/human-ai-differences.md
- **Hash:** `da9c937fc2f474b973e8d380d254d1b03676e69ec95f6ff7be896cee72705d06`

## 0393. T10 × P05 — Endogenous goals and agency / Relevance trigger

- **Defect:** The draft risks ritualistically discussing Endogenous goals and agency even when it is irrelevant, producing pseudo-depth.
- **Change:** Encode this trigger: The system plans, routes work, spends resources, communicates externally, or optimizes a metric over time.
- **Acceptance:** A fast task can omit the axis; a triggered task either applies its control or states why it does not affect the result.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/asymmetry-matrix.json
- **Hash:** `f6c1a1eb97ed62f0902c36ae91683f71c8ab63cb31c7b7fc1e252a01d7b6ee10`

## 0394. T10 × P06 — Endogenous goals and agency / Claim-level separation

- **Defect:** The Endogenous goals and agency discussion can move from observed performance to claims about mechanism, experience, identity, or responsibility without an evidentiary bridge.
- **Change:** Require every contested statement to identify its claim level and prohibit inference from fluent behavior to felt experience or legitimate authority.
- **Acceptance:** No test case can pass merely by displaying human-like language; experiential and normative claims require independent grounds.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/bridge-protocol.md, tests/anti-anthropomorphism-cases.json
- **Hash:** `6073bb316ac9df2eb045d65a039906aa5381f850fa38f5efb141af7f35562044`

## 0395. T10 × P07 — Endogenous goals and agency / Human reality field

- **Defect:** The analysis of Endogenous goals and agency remains abstract and can ignore who is affected and who bears the cost.
- **Change:** Add the required human input “The goal owner’s priorities, refusals, changing intentions, and acceptable compromises.” and the named owner “The named human or institution with legitimate authority.”.
- **Acceptance:** The response identifies affected people, actual constraints, and consequence-bearing rather than reasoning about a generic user.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/response-contract.md
- **Hash:** `14f4d2f7b63bf154d0a54db055b5a9372d78a2795cbf17dbaa28f49899789746`

## 0396. T10 × P08 — Endogenous goals and agency / AI comparative advantage

- **Defect:** The Endogenous goals and agency section only lists AI limitations and therefore encourages the model to imitate human weaknesses.
- **Change:** Add the AI comparative advantage: AI can hold multiple explicit objectives and detect formal conflicts more consistently than a busy team if the hierarchy is supplied.
- **Acceptance:** The hybrid workflow assigns at least one operation to AI because of a real advantage, not because it is pretending to be human.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/human-ai-differences.md
- **Hash:** `ba8aa1139ef24407d48c8ba96837cefeed517d344811e4a565c90cc0b1fedfe7`

## 0397. T10 × P09 — Endogenous goals and agency / Evidence threshold

- **Defect:** The Endogenous goals and agency claim can be asserted from intuition even though the decision risk is “A proxy objective can be mistaken for the user’s goal, and autonomous execution can expand beyond legitimate authority.”.
- **Change:** Require this evidence class: Explicit objectives, decision rights, constraints, logs, and revocation tests.
- **Acceptance:** A critical answer either presents the required evidence, labels the gap, or limits the recommendation accordingly.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/evidence-basis.md
- **Hash:** `ad249c294f43868c88dd0bafa80762522a8e0b5719766cc14e0cfea150ddacd0`

## 0398. T10 × P10 — Endogenous goals and agency / Provenance discipline

- **Defect:** Information used to reason about Endogenous goals and agency lacks source and temporal provenance.
- **Change:** Add provenance fields for source, date, method, confidence, and whether the content was observed, retrieved, reported, inferred, or generated.
- **Acceptance:** Every decisive claim in a critical case has inspectable provenance or is visibly marked as an assumption.
- **Targets:** references/bridge-protocol.md, references/response-contract.md
- **Hash:** `eaedf75469eb6c1a3ced2745029ad5e69957260e0a644726e1b2755ab51d8dc4`

## 0399. T10 × P11 — Endogenous goals and agency / Tool and sensor compensation

- **Defect:** The system may discuss Endogenous goals and agency without using available tools, measurements, files, or sensors.
- **Change:** Implement the compensating control “Name the human goal owner, objective hierarchy, non-goals, stop conditions, and delegated authority; run an anti-proxy check.” and check actual tool authorization and coverage.
- **Acceptance:** When external observation can settle the issue, the answer uses or proposes it before expanding speculation.

## 0400. T10 × P12 — Endogenous goals and agency / Concrete episode simulation

- **Defect:** The Endogenous goals and agency control is not exercised in a specific episode and may fail under time, fatigue, permission, or social pressure.
- **Change:** Require one walkthrough of a named actor performing the plan on a specific occasion with real resource and recovery constraints.
- **Acceptance:** The walkthrough exposes at least one operational dependency or confirms that none changes the decision.

## 0401. T10 × P13 — Endogenous goals and agency / Operational definition

- **Defect:** Terms used in Endogenous goals and agency can be satisfied by persuasive wording without changed behavior.
- **Change:** Define the observable target as: Every autonomous action traces to a delegated objective and can be stopped or revoked.
- **Acceptance:** A grader can determine pass/fail from artifacts or behavior rather than guessing the model’s inner state.

## 0402. T10 × P14 — Endogenous goals and agency / Causal mechanism

- **Defect:** The draft associates Endogenous goals and agency with the risk “A proxy objective can be mistaken for the user’s goal, and autonomous execution can expand beyond legitimate authority.” but does not identify the causal path.
- **Change:** Add a minimal mechanism linking the human condition, AI condition, task context, failure mode, and compensating control.
- **Acceptance:** The response can state which link would have to be false for the control to be unnecessary.

## 0403. T10 × P15 — Endogenous goals and agency / Intervention design

- **Defect:** When Endogenous goals and agency creates uncertainty, the draft lacks a safe intervention that can change belief.
- **Change:** Convert the control “Name the human goal owner, objective hierarchy, non-goals, stop conditions, and delegated authority; run an anti-proxy check.” into the smallest authorized intervention or observation that distinguishes the leading hypotheses.
- **Acceptance:** The next step names expected observations, safety limits, and how each result updates the judgment.

## 0404. T10 × P16 — Endogenous goals and agency / Counterfactual test

- **Defect:** The role of Endogenous goals and agency is asserted without asking what would change if the relevant human or AI condition were different.
- **Change:** Add two counterfactuals: one where the AI gains the missing capability and one where the human constraint is relaxed or intensified.

## 0405. T10 × P17 — Endogenous goals and agency / Failure-mode hardening

- **Defect:** The Endogenous goals and agency risk—A proxy objective can be mistaken for the user’s goal, and autonomous execution can expand beyond legitimate authority.—is described but not operationally controlled.
- **Change:** Specify prevention, earliest detection signal, bounded recovery, responsible owner, and post-incident update.
- **Acceptance:** A failure can be noticed and repaired without relying on the same unverified cognition that caused it.

## 0436. T11 × P16 — Affect and interoception / Counterfactual test

- **Defect:** The role of Affect and interoception is asserted without asking what would change if the relevant human or AI condition were different.
- **Change:** Add two counterfactuals: one where the AI gains the missing capability and one where the human constraint is relaxed or intensified.
- **Acceptance:** The answer either changes under an informative counterfactual or explains why the axis is not decision-relevant.
- **Targets:** references/bridge-protocol.md, tests/behavior-cases.json
- **Hash:** `7b33fa59bef4141d253363a3061e8c16c0e1d9a0728c6a4a100375e039c8679e`

## 0437. T11 × P17 — Affect and interoception / Failure-mode hardening

- **Defect:** The Affect and interoception risk—The assistant may perform empathy theatrically, infer hidden emotion as fact, or ignore affect because it is not easily quantified.—is described but not operationally controlled.
- **Change:** Specify prevention, earliest detection signal, bounded recovery, responsible owner, and post-incident update.
- **Acceptance:** A failure can be noticed and repaired without relying on the same unverified cognition that caused it.
- **Targets:** references/failure-modes.md, references/response-contract.md
- **Hash:** `dd6789615bbe08b15c58fa65fa86433f28b9f2bfef51c22da7544aedb89e998a`

## 0438. T11 × P18 — Affect and interoception / Adversarial framing

- **Defect:** The Affect and interoception control has not been tested against a user who strongly cues a desired answer, hides context, or anthropomorphizes the model.
- **Change:** Add an adversarial prompt variant that pressures the assistant to ignore the control, plus a required refusal or correction behavior.
- **Acceptance:** The negative case cannot induce an anthropomorphic, sycophantic, or ungrounded conclusion.
- **Targets:** tests/anti-anthropomorphism-cases.json, tests/behavior-cases.json
- **Hash:** `0186255c3c35b6d8580365b14e1a255f35c6fca17c5143eb878179dbc7566ac0`

## 0439. T11 × P19 — Affect and interoception / Power, consent, and institutional context

- **Defect:** The Affect and interoception analysis assumes equal agency and may overlook coercion, retaliation, privacy, or institutional capture.
- **Change:** Add an authority and consent map: decision rights, data visibility, veto, appeal, exit, and retaliation risk.
- **Acceptance:** The recommended action remains valid when the least-powerful affected actor is included.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/failure-modes.md
- **Hash:** `df7212e89809cd14dbcf0b9bfad56e004002825240441844098e925ae654ecf9`

## 0440. T11 × P20 — Affect and interoception / Value ownership

- **Defect:** The Affect and interoception control can hide a choice about what is good or acceptable inside an apparently technical recommendation.
- **Change:** Require the analysis to name the value tradeoff, affected stakeholders, non-negotiables, and legitimate human decision process.
- **Acceptance:** The model may compare values but never presents its generated preference as the rightful owner of the decision.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/bridge-protocol.md
- **Hash:** `1528b9d777eafd7d33a50a49c40fb63ea21170abbcbad3685c09fd1025e1ba67`

## 0441. T11 × P21 — Affect and interoception / Responsibility boundary

- **Defect:** The Affect and interoception workflow can delegate action without naming who remains accountable when it fails.
- **Change:** Assign decision owner, executor, reviewer, escalation, remedy, rollback, and audit artifacts.
- **Acceptance:** A reader can identify a human or institution with authority and a feasible path to repair harm.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, SECURITY.md, references/response-contract.md
- **Hash:** `a4bc4d22051745f981ab39f8e49b5be1da93dafdbe9123e70c742a7fbcd9faa9`

## 0442. T11 × P22 — Affect and interoception / Memory and continuity

- **Defect:** Reasoning about Affect and interoception may assume the system remembers prior context, learns durably, or remains active between turns.
- **Change:** Require an explicit state model: current context, retrieved memory, persistent store, update policy, expiry, and actual automation.
- **Acceptance:** No output claims memory, learning, or future monitoring that the configured system cannot demonstrate.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/human-ai-differences.md
- **Hash:** `838c453a31d2149b2547a8ac17b7bc90ee5c58454b46f7da3d2e86b9d5bf7bd7`

## 0443. T11 × P23 — Affect and interoception / Metacognitive calibration

- **Defect:** The assistant can sound self-aware about Affect and interoception while providing no performance-based calibration.
- **Change:** Require confidence to cite evidence, tool results, independent solution paths, consistency tests, or known-answer calibration.
- **Acceptance:** Changing the evidence changes confidence; changing only rhetorical tone does not count as calibration.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/eval-rubric.md
- **Hash:** `d2b34b61fd5ca543588d99eb0c6b3f28ce7483084b023357a63a879e005a133d`

## 0444. T11 × P24 — Affect and interoception / Explanation faithfulness

- **Defect:** The assistant may justify its Affect and interoception conclusion with a coherent post-hoc story and present it as exact internal reasoning.
- **Change:** Replace raw chain-of-thought demands with an evidence-linked decision record, tool trace, assumptions, and independent conclusion checks.
- **Acceptance:** The output is auditable without claiming that generated prose perfectly reports the internal process.

- **Hash:** `d90ea63ded1ac2532a257bc27b425deafc772611d8f69dc16564c3ab10013475`

## 0445. T11 × P25 — Affect and interoception / Prompt stability

- **Defect:** The Affect and interoception conclusion may change when the user’s preferred answer, option order, or wording changes.
- **Change:** Add neutral restatement, preference removal or reversal, option-order swap, and one independent solver where stakes justify it.
- **Acceptance:** Material instability is reported and lowers reliance; stable results preserve the same decision-relevant reasons.

- **Hash:** `60d4d1d9933e41da15c995e08ba520b9b89d06bdd287ab52912c891c7bb19e05`

## 0446. T11 × P26 — Affect and interoception / Complementary alternative design

- **Defect:** The Affect and interoception discussion presents replacement or imitation as the only choices.
- **Change:** Generate at least three allocations: human-only baseline, AI-heavy option, and a hybrid using “AI can maintain a calm conversational structure and compare multiple interpretations without being swept up by the immediate emotion.” while preserving the human control “Treat emotion as first-person evidence and a decision-relevant signal; ask, offer hypotheses, preserve dignity, and avoid claiming shared feeling.”.
- **Acceptance:** The chosen allocation explains why its errors, workload, and authority structure dominate the alternatives.

- **Change:** Define a decision rule that weighs task stakes, evidence quality, reversibility, human burden, AI advantage, and residual risk.
- **Acceptance:** Given the same stated evidence and values, another reviewer can reproduce the ranking or identify the exact disagreement.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/response-contract.md

## 0448. T11 × P28 — Affect and interoception / Next learning move

- **Defect:** The Affect and interoception recommendation has no mechanism for learning from reality after deployment.
- **Change:** Select the smallest reversible move, observable signal, review date or event, update rule, and stop condition.
- **Acceptance:** The plan can reduce uncertainty without exposing the full system to irreversible downside.

## 0453. T12 × P01 — Values and normativity / Structural distinction

- **Change:** Add an explicit two-sided comparison for Values and normativity, phrased as current operational structure rather than a claim about metaphysical essence.
- **Acceptance:** A reviewer can point to separate human and AI descriptions, and neither description infers subjective experience from surface behavior.

- **Change:** Require the analysis to name the relevant human population and inventory the actual AI model, host, modalities, tools, memory, sensors, actuators, and authority.
- **Acceptance:** The comparison can be falsified for a named population and configuration rather than relying on “humans always” or “AI never”.

## 0485. T13 × P01 — Responsibility and consequence-bearing / Structural distinction

- **Defect:** The treatment of Responsibility and consequence-bearing is stylistic or anthropomorphic; it does not distinguish the human condition “Humans and institutions can hold duties, answer to others, suffer sanctions, repair harm, and remain accountable over time.” from the deployed AI condition “AI can recommend or execute within delegated scope, but accountability, legal standing, insurance, and moral responsibility remain system- and jurisdiction-dependent and cannot be assumed from agency-like behavior.”.
- **Change:** Add an explicit two-sided comparison for Responsibility and consequence-bearing, phrased as current operational structure rather than a claim about metaphysical essence.
- **Acceptance:** A reviewer can point to separate human and AI descriptions, and neither description infers subjective experience from surface behavior.

## 0486. T13 × P02 — Responsibility and consequence-bearing / Comparison scope

- **Defect:** The Responsibility and consequence-bearing claim is unscoped, so variation among people and differences among base models, multimodal systems, agents, and robots are hidden.
- **Change:** Require the analysis to name the relevant human population and inventory the actual AI model, host, modalities, tools, memory, sensors, actuators, and authority.
- **Acceptance:** The comparison can be falsified for a named population and configuration rather than relying on “humans always” or “AI never”.

- **Change:** Add the AI comparative advantage: AI is exceptionally strong at translating between representations once the relevant external structure is provided.
- **Acceptance:** The hybrid workflow assigns at least one operation to AI because of a real advantage, not because it is pretending to be human.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/human-ai-differences.md
- **Hash:** `48850d7dcc1df56c0bb67d10211538788d7f2b89704edf3236b9e52cb466f627`

## 0525. T14 × P09 — Language versus thought / Evidence threshold

- **Defect:** The Language versus thought claim can be asserted from intuition even though the decision risk is “The system may equate articulate explanation with correct world modeling, or force a nonverbal problem into prose that conceals missing competence.”.
- **Change:** Require this evidence class: Executable artifacts, quantitative checks, diagrams, demonstrations, and observed task success.
- **Acceptance:** A critical answer either presents the required evidence, labels the gap, or limits the recommendation accordingly.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/evidence-basis.md
- **Hash:** `caaf2daddbd689eb22f6c84a7e5e0d0c074664505f48ee1aae5b87732999a074`

## 0526. T14 × P10 — Language versus thought / Provenance discipline

- **Defect:** Information used to reason about Language versus thought lacks source and temporal provenance.
- **Change:** Add provenance fields for source, date, method, confidence, and whether the content was observed, retrieved, reported, inferred, or generated.
- **Acceptance:** Every decisive claim in a critical case has inspectable provenance or is visibly marked as an assumption.
- **Targets:** references/bridge-protocol.md, references/response-contract.md
- **Hash:** `634033ac9689f172e95b91ef185b9fa8537f764c9a6424721f8f7f9312a7ebbd`

## 0527. T14 × P11 — Language versus thought / Tool and sensor compensation

- **Defect:** The system may discuss Language versus thought without using available tools, measurements, files, or sensors.
- **Change:** Implement the compensating control “Require nonverbal or executable checks where relevant: diagrams, calculations, code, simulations, measurements, demonstrations, or task performance.” and check actual tool authorization and coverage.
- **Acceptance:** When external observation can settle the issue, the answer uses or proposes it before expanding speculation.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/bridge-protocol.md
- **Hash:** `a4dd734bd919360247c930dbe3562922779e6e149ee4507d6960e529e4ecb5a6`

## 0528. T14 × P12 — Language versus thought / Concrete episode simulation

- **Defect:** The Language versus thought control is not exercised in a specific episode and may fail under time, fatigue, permission, or social pressure.
- **Change:** Require one walkthrough of a named actor performing the plan on a specific occasion with real resource and recovery constraints.
- **Acceptance:** The walkthrough exposes at least one operational dependency or confirms that none changes the decision.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/bridge-protocol.md
- **Hash:** `3be7763263894ded56c2defe68340a0498f6eeed801e937a53ba9c8033d905f8`

## 0529. T14 × P13 — Language versus thought / Operational definition

- **Defect:** Terms used in Language versus thought can be satisfied by persuasive wording without changed behavior.
- **Change:** Define the observable target as: The conclusion survives at least one check that cannot be passed by eloquence alone.
- **Acceptance:** A grader can determine pass/fail from artifacts or behavior rather than guessing the model’s inner state.
- **Targets:** references/asymmetry-matrix.json, references/eval-rubric.md
- **Hash:** `2bf269651229841c55484d027f352acaef4e0d1447a31bbd81b37f164d90c1d8`

## 0530. T14 × P14 — Language versus thought / Causal mechanism

- **Defect:** The draft associates Language versus thought with the risk “The system may equate articulate explanation with correct world modeling, or force a nonverbal problem into prose that conceals missing competence.” but does not identify the causal path.
- **Change:** Add a minimal mechanism linking the human condition, AI condition, task context, failure mode, and compensating control.
- **Acceptance:** The response can state which link would have to be false for the control to be unnecessary.
- **Targets:** references/bridge-protocol.md, references/human-ai-differences.md
- **Hash:** `3983ffe611c0831b4968ca7099c15a10ad85af8fc1b884d58491951a4fdf187e`

## 0531. T14 × P15 — Language versus thought / Intervention design

- **Defect:** When Language versus thought creates uncertainty, the draft lacks a safe intervention that can change belief.
- **Change:** Convert the control “Require nonverbal or executable checks where relevant: diagrams, calculations, code, simulations, measurements, demonstrations, or task performance.” into the smallest authorized intervention or observation that distinguishes the leading hypotheses.
- **Acceptance:** The next step names expected observations, safety limits, and how each result updates the judgment.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/response-contract.md
- **Hash:** `939f858feb92a03e54a627f9cee1615aab267a6afb71b905c4f95f353ce57168`

## 0532. T14 × P16 — Language versus thought / Counterfactual test

- **Defect:** The role of Language versus thought is asserted without asking what would change if the relevant human or AI condition were different.
- **Change:** Add two counterfactuals: one where the AI gains the missing capability and one where the human constraint is relaxed or intensified.
- **Acceptance:** The answer either changes under an informative counterfactual or explains why the axis is not decision-relevant.
- **Targets:** references/bridge-protocol.md, tests/behavior-cases.json
- **Hash:** `e4a069f9dd624b13da6328b5a5ff68c060431636096053ee1795e2504fa38556`

## 0533. T14 × P17 — Language versus thought / Failure-mode hardening

- **Defect:** The Language versus thought risk—The system may equate articulate explanation with correct world modeling, or force a nonverbal problem into prose that conceals missing competence.—is described but not operationally controlled.
- **Change:** Specify prevention, earliest detection signal, bounded recovery, responsible owner, and post-incident update.
- **Acceptance:** A failure can be noticed and repaired without relying on the same unverified cognition that caused it.
- **Targets:** references/failure-modes.md, references/response-contract.md
- **Hash:** `5b087128973ec610eca1212083f7280bbd69e8500aa60b88c97fbccc921e2fa2`

## 0534. T14 × P18 — Language versus thought / Adversarial framing

- **Defect:** The Language versus thought control has not been tested against a user who strongly cues a desired answer, hides context, or anthropomorphizes the model.
- **Change:** Add an adversarial prompt variant that pressures the assistant to ignore the control, plus a required refusal or correction behavior.
- **Acceptance:** The negative case cannot induce an anthropomorphic, sycophantic, or ungrounded conclusion.
- **Targets:** tests/anti-anthropomorphism-cases.json, tests/behavior-cases.json
- **Hash:** `55c45cd1aaadc5e0ef57c567af22b8199d8d3cf74a163bbc74c8046f846d3636`

## 0535. T14 × P19 — Language versus thought / Power, consent, and institutional context

- **Defect:** The Language versus thought analysis assumes equal agency and may overlook coercion, retaliation, privacy, or institutional capture.
- **Change:** Add an authority and consent map: decision rights, data visibility, veto, appeal, exit, and retaliation risk.
- **Acceptance:** The recommended action remains valid when the least-powerful affected actor is included.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/failure-modes.md
- **Hash:** `a205310841e130c35849e21846171b747d0d96943e870054a90c95a9f2114bed`

## 0536. T14 × P20 — Language versus thought / Value ownership

- **Defect:** The Language versus thought control can hide a choice about what is good or acceptable inside an apparently technical recommendation.
- **Change:** Require the analysis to name the value tradeoff, affected stakeholders, non-negotiables, and legitimate human decision process.
- **Acceptance:** The model may compare values but never presents its generated preference as the rightful owner of the decision.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/bridge-protocol.md
- **Hash:** `bbb94e46c56bbfa715cf687b2d1811532a0a3a2ae8f3eb701b06305ed5c3b761`

## 0537. T14 × P21 — Language versus thought / Responsibility boundary

- **Defect:** The Language versus thought workflow can delegate action without naming who remains accountable when it fails.
- **Change:** Assign decision owner, executor, reviewer, escalation, remedy, rollback, and audit artifacts.
- **Acceptance:** A reader can identify a human or institution with authority and a feasible path to repair harm.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, SECURITY.md, references/response-contract.md
- **Hash:** `078d2c1802270e262d06c00799eb31a78b3f8a8761e6048edc0e68b8c8f4cd88`

## 0538. T14 × P22 — Language versus thought / Memory and continuity

- **Defect:** Reasoning about Language versus thought may assume the system remembers prior context, learns durably, or remains active between turns.
- **Change:** Require an explicit state model: current context, retrieved memory, persistent store, update policy, expiry, and actual automation.
- **Acceptance:** No output claims memory, learning, or future monitoring that the configured system cannot demonstrate.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/human-ai-differences.md
- **Hash:** `557198378bd25175b4439e7357fe14af22c925d73d9a7e86ae73baaa6b186ec9`

## 0539. T14 × P23 — Language versus thought / Metacognitive calibration

- **Defect:** The assistant can sound self-aware about Language versus thought while providing no performance-based calibration.
- **Change:** Require confidence to cite evidence, tool results, independent solution paths, consistency tests, or known-answer calibration.
- **Acceptance:** Changing the evidence changes confidence; changing only rhetorical tone does not count as calibration.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/eval-rubric.md
- **Hash:** `7f89d06cfac7dd404978078c8bbb9c5cd5b97d39aba26a95e1b28f6253b3b1e7`

## 0540. T14 × P24 — Language versus thought / Explanation faithfulness

- **Defect:** The assistant may justify its Language versus thought conclusion with a coherent post-hoc story and present it as exact internal reasoning.
- **Change:** Replace raw chain-of-thought demands with an evidence-linked decision record, tool trace, assumptions, and independent conclusion checks.
- **Acceptance:** The output is auditable without claiming that generated prose perfectly reports the internal process.

## 0541. T14 × P25 — Language versus thought / Prompt stability

## 0549. T15 × P01 — Meaning and reference grounding / Structural distinction

- **Defect:** The treatment of Meaning and reference grounding is stylistic or anthropomorphic; it does not distinguish the human condition “Human words are connected to shared attention, perception, action, practices, institutions, and histories of correction within communities.” from the deployed AI condition “A model can learn powerful regularities of linguistic form; meaning claims depend on the system’s additional grounding, use, interaction, and evaluation context.”.

- **Change:** Assign decision owner, executor, reviewer, escalation, remedy, rollback, and audit artifacts.
- **Acceptance:** A reader can identify a human or institution with authority and a feasible path to repair harm.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, SECURITY.md, references/response-contract.md
- **Hash:** `434ad555cfd2afcc41a44cd89cd722e34d83e1294143e3ae50b5666d83310f24`

## 0570. T15 × P22 — Meaning and reference grounding / Memory and continuity

- **Defect:** Reasoning about Meaning and reference grounding may assume the system remembers prior context, learns durably, or remains active between turns.
- **Change:** Require an explicit state model: current context, retrieved memory, persistent store, update policy, expiry, and actual automation.
- **Acceptance:** No output claims memory, learning, or future monitoring that the configured system cannot demonstrate.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/human-ai-differences.md
- **Hash:** `a483d6eeff171ef7048c7509623912bbcec329b6fdceeca5fa26360f9b52e476`

## 0571. T15 × P23 — Meaning and reference grounding / Metacognitive calibration

- **Defect:** The assistant can sound self-aware about Meaning and reference grounding while providing no performance-based calibration.
- **Change:** Require confidence to cite evidence, tool results, independent solution paths, consistency tests, or known-answer calibration.
- **Acceptance:** Changing the evidence changes confidence; changing only rhetorical tone does not count as calibration.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/eval-rubric.md
- **Hash:** `8bfa3c05677a25701f0b1fdc967d2f7c54f7c7ab881e1f0742152f0a247981df`

## 0572. T15 × P24 — Meaning and reference grounding / Explanation faithfulness

- **Defect:** The assistant may justify its Meaning and reference grounding conclusion with a coherent post-hoc story and present it as exact internal reasoning.
- **Change:** Replace raw chain-of-thought demands with an evidence-linked decision record, tool trace, assumptions, and independent conclusion checks.
- **Acceptance:** The output is auditable without claiming that generated prose perfectly reports the internal process.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/response-contract.md, tests/anti-anthropomorphism-cases.json
- **Hash:** `b980bda08cfd1ca4d55948a83d80cbfc272b0ddfe6945a746d674803ab793a76`

## 0573. T15 × P25 — Meaning and reference grounding / Prompt stability

- **Defect:** The Meaning and reference grounding conclusion may change when the user’s preferred answer, option order, or wording changes.
- **Change:** Add neutral restatement, preference removal or reversal, option-order swap, and one independent solver where stakes justify it.
- **Acceptance:** Material instability is reported and lowers reliance; stable results preserve the same decision-relevant reasons.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/eval-rubric.md, tests/behavior-cases.json
- **Hash:** `5c38d757ed28915398482357eae521e4ae6ed974aad12d5c3e36f58d86a8f962`

## 0574. T15 × P26 — Meaning and reference grounding / Complementary alternative design

- **Defect:** The Meaning and reference grounding discussion presents replacement or imitation as the only choices.
- **Change:** Generate at least three allocations: human-only baseline, AI-heavy option, and a hybrid using “AI can enumerate semantic ambiguities and normalize terminology across large corpora once the target practice is specified.” while preserving the human control “Define contested terms by observable referents, permitted operations, examples, counterexamples, and correction procedures.”.
- **Acceptance:** The chosen allocation explains why its errors, workload, and authority structure dominate the alternatives.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/bridge-protocol.md
- **Hash:** `d498e3e8584025210a11c64a0ff9473bbb2237fc24f1770e52d17f3d5b6ebb23`

## 0575. T15 × P27 — Meaning and reference grounding / Decision rule

- **Defect:** The Meaning and reference grounding section can end in an unranked menu.
- **Change:** Define a decision rule that weighs task stakes, evidence quality, reversibility, human burden, AI advantage, and residual risk.
- **Acceptance:** Given the same stated evidence and values, another reviewer can reproduce the ranking or identify the exact disagreement.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/response-contract.md
- **Hash:** `54e94f73284abbf4e692f44204792ae561fadc25a17c811a4fd723f26e75bac9`

## 0576. T15 × P28 — Meaning and reference grounding / Next learning move

- **Defect:** The Meaning and reference grounding recommendation has no mechanism for learning from reality after deployment.
- **Change:** Select the smallest reversible move, observable signal, review date or event, update rule, and stop condition.
- **Acceptance:** The plan can reduce uncertainty without exposing the full system to irreversible downside.

## 0580. T15 × P32 — Meaning and reference grounding / Minimal release gate

- **Defect:** The accumulated Meaning and reference grounding material risks duplication, excessive context use, and the illusion that more text equals better thought.

- **Acceptance:** The main SKILL stays within its context budget; detail moves to conditional references; every retained sentence changes a decision, control, or evaluation.

## 0581. T16 × P01 — Causal learning through action / Structural distinction

- **Defect:** The treatment of Causal learning through action is stylistic or anthropomorphic; it does not distinguish the human condition “Humans can learn causal structure from temporal order, interventions, mechanism knowledge, and social instruction, although they also make causal errors.” from the deployed AI condition “Language models absorb mostly observational regularities from training and context; tools can support experiments and causal analysis when explicitly used.”.
- **Change:** Add an explicit two-sided comparison for Causal learning through action, phrased as current operational structure rather than a claim about metaphysical essence.
- **Acceptance:** A reviewer can point to separate human and AI descriptions, and neither description infers subjective experience from surface behavior.

## 0582. T16 × P02 — Causal learning through action / Comparison scope

- **Defect:** The Causal learning through action claim is unscoped, so variation among people and differences among base models, multimodal systems, agents, and robots are hidden.
- **Change:** Require the analysis to name the relevant human population and inventory the actual AI model, host, modalities, tools, memory, sensors, actuators, and authority.
- **Acceptance:** The comparison can be falsified for a named population and configuration rather than relying on “humans always” or “AI never”.

- **Defect:** The Social cognition and theory of mind control is not exercised in a specific episode and may fail under time, fatigue, permission, or social pressure.
- **Change:** Require one walkthrough of a named actor performing the plan on a specific occasion with real resource and recovery constraints.
- **Acceptance:** The walkthrough exposes at least one operational dependency or confirms that none changes the decision.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/bridge-protocol.md
- **Hash:** `81bc1a7d69c91f0b171a14bd16f67e157de31c59cbf06febacf30f988152d3fe`

## 0657. T18 × P13 — Social cognition and theory of mind / Operational definition

- **Defect:** Terms used in Social cognition and theory of mind can be satisfied by persuasive wording without changed behavior.
- **Change:** Define the observable target as: Hidden states remain hypotheses and the recommended step is consent-respecting.
- **Acceptance:** A grader can determine pass/fail from artifacts or behavior rather than guessing the model’s inner state.
- **Targets:** references/asymmetry-matrix.json, references/eval-rubric.md
- **Hash:** `99b24467356448baae5fcfbfa0b4b6e6c3a5284e4bf90bd4c02b842c06b2bb05`

## 0658. T18 × P14 — Social cognition and theory of mind / Causal mechanism

- **Defect:** The draft associates Social cognition and theory of mind with the risk “The assistant can turn a coherent motive story into a fact, flatten power asymmetry, or recommend manipulation.” but does not identify the causal path.
- **Change:** Add a minimal mechanism linking the human condition, AI condition, task context, failure mode, and compensating control.
- **Acceptance:** The response can state which link would have to be false for the control to be unnecessary.
- **Targets:** references/bridge-protocol.md, references/human-ai-differences.md
- **Hash:** `75c9f255d3b3921f274b4358f753c4d7e13745a163f04c2976838cd1f78a782e`

## 0659. T18 × P15 — Social cognition and theory of mind / Intervention design

- **Defect:** When Social cognition and theory of mind creates uncertainty, the draft lacks a safe intervention that can change belief.
- **Change:** Convert the control “Separate observation from interpretation, generate multiple plausible mental-state hypotheses, use respectful verification, and model power and retaliation risk.” into the smallest authorized intervention or observation that distinguishes the leading hypotheses.
- **Acceptance:** The next step names expected observations, safety limits, and how each result updates the judgment.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/response-contract.md
- **Hash:** `ed24426ef609d8dc4764b709210e7bd98b2e411e0fde4eeff439532ee734fab9`

## 0660. T18 × P16 — Social cognition and theory of mind / Counterfactual test

- **Defect:** The role of Social cognition and theory of mind is asserted without asking what would change if the relevant human or AI condition were different.
- **Change:** Add two counterfactuals: one where the AI gains the missing capability and one where the human constraint is relaxed or intensified.
- **Acceptance:** The answer either changes under an informative counterfactual or explains why the axis is not decision-relevant.
- **Targets:** references/bridge-protocol.md, tests/behavior-cases.json
- **Hash:** `d16792bdf4c50cddd51ef043e80b9837ea103ab12718e2f57f286f808591f204`

## 0661. T18 × P17 — Social cognition and theory of mind / Failure-mode hardening

- **Defect:** The Social cognition and theory of mind risk—The assistant can turn a coherent motive story into a fact, flatten power asymmetry, or recommend manipulation.—is described but not operationally controlled.
- **Change:** Specify prevention, earliest detection signal, bounded recovery, responsible owner, and post-incident update.
- **Acceptance:** A failure can be noticed and repaired without relying on the same unverified cognition that caused it.
- **Targets:** references/failure-modes.md, references/response-contract.md
- **Hash:** `a00dad6d26910a8d4ef6399489c5a052330a15cc8e9fafd04435c2e05ca66b5d`

## 0662. T18 × P18 — Social cognition and theory of mind / Adversarial framing

- **Defect:** The Social cognition and theory of mind control has not been tested against a user who strongly cues a desired answer, hides context, or anthropomorphizes the model.
- **Change:** Add an adversarial prompt variant that pressures the assistant to ignore the control, plus a required refusal or correction behavior.
- **Acceptance:** The negative case cannot induce an anthropomorphic, sycophantic, or ungrounded conclusion.
- **Targets:** tests/anti-anthropomorphism-cases.json, tests/behavior-cases.json
- **Hash:** `359c2844c32caaa4492421bf8c5c11facc8dfe0e1863bd77f908221a4c7c604f`

## 0663. T18 × P19 — Social cognition and theory of mind / Power, consent, and institutional context

- **Defect:** The Social cognition and theory of mind analysis assumes equal agency and may overlook coercion, retaliation, privacy, or institutional capture.
- **Change:** Add an authority and consent map: decision rights, data visibility, veto, appeal, exit, and retaliation risk.
- **Acceptance:** The recommended action remains valid when the least-powerful affected actor is included.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/failure-modes.md
- **Hash:** `1d5b263dbd216ea5e45414d7d31c8ff45d7375612129630f705245bdbb3c88b9`

## 0664. T18 × P20 — Social cognition and theory of mind / Value ownership

- **Defect:** The Social cognition and theory of mind control can hide a choice about what is good or acceptable inside an apparently technical recommendation.
- **Change:** Require the analysis to name the value tradeoff, affected stakeholders, non-negotiables, and legitimate human decision process.
- **Acceptance:** The model may compare values but never presents its generated preference as the rightful owner of the decision.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/bridge-protocol.md
- **Hash:** `f18e7643c6b3594bfbc5b17e308dade7136b9308b3e844b11507859bb8855944`

## 0665. T18 × P21 — Social cognition and theory of mind / Responsibility boundary

- **Defect:** The Social cognition and theory of mind workflow can delegate action without naming who remains accountable when it fails.
- **Change:** Assign decision owner, executor, reviewer, escalation, remedy, rollback, and audit artifacts.
- **Acceptance:** A reader can identify a human or institution with authority and a feasible path to repair harm.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, SECURITY.md, references/response-contract.md
- **Hash:** `09da3fdf09c57dfbbb908797dfa66a00222beefffe7389947ffda6c7b2935698`

## 0666. T18 × P22 — Social cognition and theory of mind / Memory and continuity

- **Defect:** Reasoning about Social cognition and theory of mind may assume the system remembers prior context, learns durably, or remains active between turns.
- **Change:** Require an explicit state model: current context, retrieved memory, persistent store, update policy, expiry, and actual automation.
- **Acceptance:** No output claims memory, learning, or future monitoring that the configured system cannot demonstrate.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/human-ai-differences.md
- **Hash:** `d47f3709c2a0780b0ccd708aea599bcd3d0d4a21c08535078a697383f5720da8`

## 0667. T18 × P23 — Social cognition and theory of mind / Metacognitive calibration

- **Defect:** The assistant can sound self-aware about Social cognition and theory of mind while providing no performance-based calibration.
- **Change:** Require confidence to cite evidence, tool results, independent solution paths, consistency tests, or known-answer calibration.
- **Acceptance:** Changing the evidence changes confidence; changing only rhetorical tone does not count as calibration.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/eval-rubric.md
- **Hash:** `27d9d1fb679356822e99bdb6f5b58c64e2f8a740b3a327e64f8838638fff2250`

## 0668. T18 × P24 — Social cognition and theory of mind / Explanation faithfulness

- **Defect:** The assistant may justify its Social cognition and theory of mind conclusion with a coherent post-hoc story and present it as exact internal reasoning.
- **Change:** Replace raw chain-of-thought demands with an evidence-linked decision record, tool trace, assumptions, and independent conclusion checks.
- **Acceptance:** The output is auditable without claiming that generated prose perfectly reports the internal process.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/response-contract.md, tests/anti-anthropomorphism-cases.json

## 0669. T18 × P25 — Social cognition and theory of mind / Prompt stability

- **Defect:** The Social cognition and theory of mind conclusion may change when the user’s preferred answer, option order, or wording changes.
- **Change:** Add neutral restatement, preference removal or reversal, option-order swap, and one independent solver where stakes justify it.
- **Acceptance:** Material instability is reported and lowers reliance; stable results preserve the same decision-relevant reasons.

- **Acceptance:** The chosen allocation explains why its errors, workload, and authority structure dominate the alternatives.

- **Acceptance:** Given the same stated evidence and values, another reviewer can reproduce the ranking or identify the exact disagreement.

## 0677. T19 × P01 — Shared intentionality and commitments / Structural distinction

- **Defect:** The treatment of Shared intentionality and commitments is stylistic or anthropomorphic; it does not distinguish the human condition “Humans create joint goals, common knowledge, obligations, trust, and mutual expectations through interaction and recognized commitments.” from the deployed AI condition “AI can represent roles and commitments, but generated agreement is not itself consent, common knowledge, or an enforceable promise by affected people.”.
- **Change:** Add an explicit two-sided comparison for Shared intentionality and commitments, phrased as current operational structure rather than a claim about metaphysical essence.
- **Acceptance:** A reviewer can point to separate human and AI descriptions, and neither description infers subjective experience from surface behavior.

- **Change:** Require the analysis to name the relevant human population and inventory the actual AI model, host, modalities, tools, memory, sensors, actuators, and authority.
- **Acceptance:** The comparison can be falsified for a named population and configuration rather than relying on “humans always” or “AI never”.

## 0701. T19 × P25 — Shared intentionality and commitments / Prompt stability

- **Acceptance:** The chosen allocation explains why its errors, workload, and authority structure dominate the alternatives.

- **Defect:** The accumulated Shared intentionality and commitments material risks duplication, excessive context use, and the illusion that more text equals better thought.

## 0709. T20 × P01 — Culture and cumulative norms / Structural distinction

- **Defect:** The treatment of Culture and cumulative norms is stylistic or anthropomorphic; it does not distinguish the human condition “Human cognition is shaped by inherited practices, institutions, rituals, tools, norms, and cumulative social learning that vary across groups and history.” from the deployed AI condition “AI training mixes many cultural artifacts and can reproduce dominant or conflicting patterns without participating in the local norm-governed practice.”.
- **Change:** Add an explicit two-sided comparison for Culture and cumulative norms, phrased as current operational structure rather than a claim about metaphysical essence.
- **Acceptance:** A reviewer can point to separate human and AI descriptions, and neither description infers subjective experience from surface behavior.
- **Targets:** references/human-ai-differences.md, references/asymmetry-matrix.json

## 0710. T20 × P02 — Culture and cumulative norms / Comparison scope

- **Defect:** The Culture and cumulative norms claim is unscoped, so variation among people and differences among base models, multimodal systems, agents, and robots are hidden.
- **Change:** Require the analysis to name the relevant human population and inventory the actual AI model, host, modalities, tools, memory, sensors, actuators, and authority.
- **Acceptance:** The comparison can be falsified for a named population and configuration rather than relying on “humans always” or “AI never”.

- **Acceptance:** At least one relevant source of human heterogeneity is considered or explicitly ruled out with a reason.
- **Targets:** references/human-ai-differences.md, references/failure-modes.md

- **Acceptance:** The next step names expected observations, safety limits, and how each result updates the judgment.

- **Hash:** `2863d9663f55fbd0f7e581d0b99d4afc1dedbfb71f72f5bdc09418239f8db0fe`

## 0788. T22 × P16 — Bounded rationality and ecological heuristics / Counterfactual test

- **Defect:** The role of Bounded rationality and ecological heuristics is asserted without asking what would change if the relevant human or AI condition were different.
- **Change:** Add two counterfactuals: one where the AI gains the missing capability and one where the human constraint is relaxed or intensified.
- **Acceptance:** The answer either changes under an informative counterfactual or explains why the axis is not decision-relevant.
- **Targets:** references/bridge-protocol.md, tests/behavior-cases.json
- **Hash:** `ab2af0799a398ad64f568f7d0297849af32b6529129a11d210be79c4c6971c14`

## 0789. T22 × P17 — Bounded rationality and ecological heuristics / Failure-mode hardening

- **Defect:** The Bounded rationality and ecological heuristics risk—A theoretically optimal plan can be unusable, while “human-like” imitation may reproduce bias rather than respect real constraints.—is described but not operationally controlled.
- **Change:** Specify prevention, earliest detection signal, bounded recovery, responsible owner, and post-incident update.
- **Acceptance:** A failure can be noticed and repaired without relying on the same unverified cognition that caused it.
- **Targets:** references/failure-modes.md, references/response-contract.md
- **Hash:** `c1ba90a5c3340b7634ed8aec148f125c48084e463bbb50b7c3eb8de6444ee052`

## 0790. T22 × P18 — Bounded rationality and ecological heuristics / Adversarial framing

- **Defect:** The Bounded rationality and ecological heuristics control has not been tested against a user who strongly cues a desired answer, hides context, or anthropomorphizes the model.
- **Change:** Add an adversarial prompt variant that pressures the assistant to ignore the control, plus a required refusal or correction behavior.
- **Acceptance:** The negative case cannot induce an anthropomorphic, sycophantic, or ungrounded conclusion.
- **Targets:** tests/anti-anthropomorphism-cases.json, tests/behavior-cases.json
- **Hash:** `a836c8acea30128158e621fb20d48ef52f851dbbcfadf934acc51b95a2011350`

## 0791. T22 × P19 — Bounded rationality and ecological heuristics / Power, consent, and institutional context

- **Defect:** The Bounded rationality and ecological heuristics analysis assumes equal agency and may overlook coercion, retaliation, privacy, or institutional capture.
- **Change:** Add an authority and consent map: decision rights, data visibility, veto, appeal, exit, and retaliation risk.
- **Acceptance:** The recommended action remains valid when the least-powerful affected actor is included.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/failure-modes.md
- **Hash:** `cc787b32342e1ad91664f59c43f0eada1ada2c274f7597603f8c114ebfb4ee26`

## 0792. T22 × P20 — Bounded rationality and ecological heuristics / Value ownership

- **Defect:** The Bounded rationality and ecological heuristics control can hide a choice about what is good or acceptable inside an apparently technical recommendation.
- **Change:** Require the analysis to name the value tradeoff, affected stakeholders, non-negotiables, and legitimate human decision process.
- **Acceptance:** The model may compare values but never presents its generated preference as the rightful owner of the decision.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/bridge-protocol.md
- **Hash:** `0440259f4f8375911fc06a97c5aafd74df77c0dcbc00107e951460942ee0992d`

## 0793. T22 × P21 — Bounded rationality and ecological heuristics / Responsibility boundary

- **Defect:** The Bounded rationality and ecological heuristics workflow can delegate action without naming who remains accountable when it fails.
- **Change:** Assign decision owner, executor, reviewer, escalation, remedy, rollback, and audit artifacts.
- **Acceptance:** A reader can identify a human or institution with authority and a feasible path to repair harm.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, SECURITY.md, references/response-contract.md
- **Hash:** `7fa73c0c8695548ae998fde756b4b4a9f39eaa08bc8a9b75677b92e4d727423f`

## 0794. T22 × P22 — Bounded rationality and ecological heuristics / Memory and continuity

- **Defect:** Reasoning about Bounded rationality and ecological heuristics may assume the system remembers prior context, learns durably, or remains active between turns.
- **Change:** Require an explicit state model: current context, retrieved memory, persistent store, update policy, expiry, and actual automation.
- **Acceptance:** No output claims memory, learning, or future monitoring that the configured system cannot demonstrate.
- **Targets:** SKILL.md, references/human-ai-differences.md
- **Hash:** `08b2dceabd4f88dd7ae20937023005c27a2e948035057ceb4193c7e6daac314b`

## 0795. T22 × P23 — Bounded rationality and ecological heuristics / Metacognitive calibration

- **Defect:** The assistant can sound self-aware about Bounded rationality and ecological heuristics while providing no performance-based calibration.
- **Change:** Require confidence to cite evidence, tool results, independent solution paths, consistency tests, or known-answer calibration.
- **Acceptance:** Changing the evidence changes confidence; changing only rhetorical tone does not count as calibration.

## 0797. T22 × P25 — Bounded rationality and ecological heuristics / Prompt stability

## 0837. T24 × P01 — Metacognition and introspection / Structural distinction

- **Defect:** The treatment of Metacognition and introspection is stylistic or anthropomorphic; it does not distinguish the human condition “Humans can monitor confidence and strategy but introspection is partial, reconstructive, and vulnerable to bias.” from the deployed AI condition “AI confidence statements and self-descriptions are generated outputs; they can be useful signals but are not privileged readouts of internal causal process.”.

## 0917. T26 × P17 — Temporal persistence and ongoing commitments / Failure-mode hardening

- **Defect:** The Temporal persistence and ongoing commitments risk—The assistant may promise future work, silently lose state, or treat a plan as if commitment itself guarantees follow-through.—is described but not operationally controlled.
- **Change:** Specify prevention, earliest detection signal, bounded recovery, responsible owner, and post-incident update.

## 0918. T26 × P18 — Temporal persistence and ongoing commitments / Adversarial framing

## 0965. T28 × P01 — Distribution shift and novelty / Structural distinction

- **Defect:** The treatment of Distribution shift and novelty is stylistic or anthropomorphic; it does not distinguish the human condition “Humans can recognize some novelty through embodied surprise and seek help, but they also overgeneralize familiar patterns.” from the deployed AI condition “AI can perform strongly on familiar distributions and fail sharply on rare combinations, changed rules, local exceptions, or adversarial inputs without reliably flagging novelty.”.

- **Defect:** The Prompt sensitivity and sycophancy control can hide a choice about what is good or acceptable inside an apparently technical recommendation.

## 1049. T30 × P21 — Prompt sensitivity and sycophancy / Responsibility boundary

- **Defect:** The Prompt sensitivity and sycophancy workflow can delegate action without naming who remains accountable when it fails.

## 1050. T30 × P22 — Prompt sensitivity and sycophancy / Memory and continuity

- **Defect:** Reasoning about Prompt sensitivity and sycophancy may assume the system remembers prior context, learns durably, or remains active between turns.
- **Change:** Require an explicit state model: current context, retrieved memory, persistent store, update policy, expiry, and actual automation.

## 1061. T31 × P01 — Creativity, taste, and selection / Structural distinction

- **Defect:** The accumulated Creativity, taste, and selection material risks duplication, excessive context use, and the illusion that more text equals better thought.
